It can also complete a project plugin manifest by adding disabled catalog entries and filling missing runtime crate/target-mode data before export planning; `ExportBuildPlan::from_project_manifest(...)` now performs that builtin completion before linked crate calculation and SourceTemplate plugin-code projection, so builtin runtime crates and target modes do not have to be repeated in `zircon-project.toml`. The builtin plugin catalog is now descriptor-derived through `RuntimePluginDescriptor::builtin_catalog()` instead of hand-written package manifests, with `builtin_catalog/core_rows.rs` chaining runtime and content row groups before asset, render, and language rows, `builtin_catalog/core_classification/` separating runtime foundation and content/tool descriptor classification, `builtin_catalog/asset_rows/` separating model, media, and pipeline importer row groups, `builtin_catalog/importer_classification/` applying that same split to importer maturity, capability-status metadata, and importer primary-capability mapping, `builtin_catalog/language/` keeping the ZrVM language row and classification metadata under one language owner, `builtin_catalog/augmentation/` separating category assignment from extra package capability attachment, `builtin_catalog/net_features/` separating Net optional-feature rows from manifest construction, `builtin_catalog/particles_features/` applying the same split to Particles physics, animation-control, and GPU-simulation feature rows, `builtin_catalog/sound_features/` keeping Sound feature rows, dependency rows, runtime/editor module crate names, and shared manifest construction out of the root optional-feature dispatcher, and `builtin_catalog/rendering_features/` doing the same for the eight Rendering optional-feature rows without changing render runtime execution ownership. This is deliberately higher level than a native library symbol: source-template exports, library-embedded exports, and future dynamic library loaders all converge on the same descriptor and registry contract.

Feature-manifest validation is also folder-backed. `feature_validation.rs` keeps the internal feature-manifest entry surface and provider package-id entry surface, while `feature_validation/identity.rs` owns feature id field/namespace diagnostics, display-name field diagnostics, owner plugin-id field/token diagnostics, and owner-prefix diagnostics before capability, dependency, module, and default-packaging validation run. `feature_validation/shape.rs` keeps the internal helper owner surface, while `shape/field.rs`, `shape/namespace.rs`, and `shape/token.rs` separately own trimmed field, dot-namespace, and lowercase owner/provider token diagnostics consumed by feature identity, capability, dependency, provider-package, module, and package-embedded feature validation. Namespace validation is split so `shape/namespace.rs` keeps namespace entry dispatch, `shape/namespace/segments.rs` owns segment-rule dispatch, `shape/namespace/segments/count.rs` owns minimum two-segment diagnostics, and `shape/namespace/segments/tokens.rs` owns lowercase segment diagnostics through the shared package-token predicate. Token validation is itself split so `shape/token.rs` keeps token rule orchestration, `shape/token/start.rs` owns lowercase-start diagnostics, `shape/token/charset.rs` owns lowercase ASCII letters/digits/underscore character-set diagnostics, and `shape/token/underscore.rs` owns trailing/repeated underscore diagnostics.

Feature provider validation follows the same owner/child split: `feature_validation/provider.rs` owns `provider_package_id` field and token diagnostics consumed by native feature manifests and provider overrides, while `feature_validation.rs` keeps the internal provider entry surface.

Feature capability validation follows that same owner/child split: `feature_validation/capabilities.rs` owns the feature-level capability entry point, `feature_validation/capabilities/presence.rs` owns empty-list diagnostics, `feature_validation/capabilities/rows.rs` owns row traversal, `feature_validation/capabilities/rows/state.rs` owns seen-capability state creation, `feature_validation/capabilities/row.rs` owns per-row validation order, `feature_validation/capabilities/row/field.rs` owns field adapter dispatch, `feature_validation/capabilities/row/namespace.rs` owns namespace adapter dispatch, `feature_validation/capabilities/row/uniqueness.rs` owns duplicate-capability adapter dispatch, and `feature_validation/capabilities/uniqueness.rs` owns duplicate capability diagnostics.

Feature dependency validation follows the same shape: `feature_validation/dependencies.rs` owns the feature-level dependency entry point, `feature_validation/dependencies/presence.rs` owns empty-list diagnostics, and `feature_validation/dependencies/rows.rs` owns dependency list traversal plus per-row validation order. Row state is split under that traversal owner: `feature_validation/dependencies/rows/pairs.rs` owns duplicate-pair seen-state adaptation into `dependencies/pairs.rs`, while `feature_validation/dependencies/rows/primary.rs` owns primary dependency count accumulation plus final count dispatch into `dependencies/primary_count.rs`. `dependencies/row.rs` owns row-shape dispatch order, `dependencies/row/provider.rs` owns provider id field/token diagnostics, `dependencies/row/capability.rs` owns dependency capability field/namespace diagnostics, and `dependencies/pairs.rs`, `dependencies/owner.rs`, plus `dependencies/primary_count.rs` keep the duplicate-pair, primary-owner, and exactly-one-primary rules.

Shared module validation is folder-backed under `module_validation.rs`, with package and feature module rows consuming the same internal owner surface. `module_validation/names.rs` owns the shared module-name entry point, `module_validation/names/shape.rs` owns caller-provided field and namespace validation dispatch, `module_validation/names/owner_prefix.rs` owns package/feature owner-prefix diagnostics, `module_validation/names/kind_suffix.rs` owns runtime/editor suffix diagnostics, and `module_validation/names/uniqueness.rs` owns package/feature-local duplicate-name diagnostics plus seen insertion. `module_validation/capabilities.rs` owns the shared capability entry point, `module_validation/capabilities/presence.rs` owns empty capability-list diagnostics, `module_validation/capabilities/rows.rs` owns capability traversal, `module_validation/capabilities/rows/state.rs` owns seen-capability state creation, `module_validation/capabilities/row.rs` owns per-capability validation order, `module_validation/capabilities/row/kind_prefix.rs` owns runtime/editor capability-prefix diagnostics, and `module_validation/capabilities/row/uniqueness.rs` owns duplicate capability diagnostics plus seen insertion. `module_validation/target_modes.rs` owns the shared target-mode entry point, `module_validation/target_modes/presence.rs` owns empty target-mode diagnostics, `module_validation/target_modes/rows.rs` owns target-mode traversal, `module_validation/target_modes/rows/state.rs` owns seen-target state creation, `module_validation/target_modes/row.rs` owns per-target validation order, `module_validation/target_modes/row/uniqueness.rs` owns duplicate target diagnostics plus seen insertion, `module_validation/target_modes/row/editor_host.rs` owns editor-only `EditorHost` diagnostics, and `module_validation/target_modes/row/coverage.rs` owns optional package supported-target coverage diagnostics. `module_validation/crate_name.rs` owns the shared module crate-name entry point, `module_validation/crate_name/shape.rs` owns caller-provided field validation dispatch, `module_validation/crate_name/token.rs` owns `zircon_plugin_` prefix and lowercase token diagnostics, and `module_validation/crate_name/underscore.rs` owns trailing/repeated underscore diagnostics.

Feature module validation is folder-backed as well: `feature_validation/modules.rs` owns the feature-level module entry point, `feature_validation/modules/rows.rs` owns module list traversal, `feature_validation/modules/rows/state.rs` owns seen-name state creation, while `feature_validation/modules/row.rs` owns per-module validation order. Row adapters are split by shared-rule family: `feature_validation/modules/row/identity.rs` owns identity rule dispatch, `feature_validation/modules/row/identity/name.rs` owns feature-id scoped module-name validation, `feature_validation/modules/row/identity/crate_name.rs` owns crate-name validation, `feature_validation/modules/row/capabilities.rs` owns feature field/namespace adaptation for shared module capability checks, and `feature_validation/modules/row/target_modes.rs` owns feature-module target-mode dispatch.

Package module validation follows that split under `package_validation/modules.rs`: `modules/field.rs` adapts package field diagnostics, `modules/rows.rs` owns package module traversal, `modules/rows/state.rs` owns seen-name state creation, `modules/row.rs` owns per-module dispatch order, `modules/row/identity.rs` owns package module identity rule dispatch, `modules/row/identity/name.rs` owns package ownership validation, `modules/row/identity/crate_name.rs` owns crate-name validation, `modules/row/capabilities.rs` owns package namespace adaptation for shared module capability checks, and `modules/row/target_modes.rs` owns package supported-target coverage adaptation.

Package-manifest validation keeps shared shape helpers under the folder-backed `package_validation/shape.rs` owner. The owner re-exports only the internal runtime-plugin helper surface, while `shape/field.rs`, `shape/token.rs`, `shape/token/charset.rs`, `shape/token/predicate.rs`, `shape/identity.rs`, `shape/identity/charset.rs`, `shape/identity/start.rs`, `shape/identity/underscore.rs`, `shape/namespace.rs`, `shape/namespace/segments.rs`, `shape/namespace/segments/count.rs`, and `shape/namespace/segments/tokens.rs` separate trimmed-field checks, package-token rule orchestration, package-token character-set checks, the shared lowercase-token predicate, package-id rule orchestration, package-id character-set checks, package-id leading-letter checks, package-id trailing/repeated underscore checks, namespace field gating, namespace segment dispatch, minimum segment diagnostics, and lowercase segment diagnostics. Descriptor validation, package validation, feature validation, and module validation all consume that same owner surface, so package ids, dependency provider ids, module crate names, feature owner ids, and capability namespaces stay on one diagnostic contract without turning `package_validation.rs` back into an implementation file.

Registration package-manifest validation now keeps package-level orchestration in `registration_report/validation.rs` and moves the descriptor/package id agreement plus display-name field checks into `registration_report/validation/identity.rs`, preserving the same validation order before layout, version, default-packaging, capability, dependency, contribution, asset-importer, embedded-feature, capability-status, and module dispatch.

Default-packaging validation is folder-backed as well: `package_validation/default_packaging.rs` owns the shared descriptor/package/feature entry point, `default_packaging/presence.rs` owns empty-list diagnostics, `default_packaging/strategies.rs` owns strategy list traversal, `default_packaging/strategies/state.rs` owns seen-strategy state creation, and `default_packaging/strategies/uniqueness.rs` owns duplicate strategy diagnostics plus seen-strategy insertion for every caller that projects export packaging fallbacks. Version validation is also folder-backed: `package_validation/versions.rs` owns semver entry dispatch, `versions/field.rs` owns non-empty/trimmed field validation, `versions/segments.rs` owns semver component traversal, `versions/segments/count.rs` owns the `MAJOR.MINOR.PATCH` segment-count diagnostic, `versions/component.rs` owns per-component rule dispatch, `versions/component/digits.rs` owns ASCII digit diagnostics, `versions/component/leading_zeroes.rs` owns leading-zero diagnostics, and `versions/component/range.rs` owns `u32` range diagnostics.

Package capability and dependency validation follows the same split under `package_validation/capability_dependencies.rs`: `capability_dependencies/capabilities.rs` owns the package capability entry point, `capabilities/presence.rs` owns empty-list diagnostics, `capabilities/rows.rs` owns package capability traversal, `capabilities/rows/state.rs` owns seen-capability state creation, `capabilities/row.rs` owns per-capability validation order, `capabilities/row/namespace.rs` owns package capability namespace diagnostics, `capabilities/row/uniqueness.rs` owns duplicate-check adapter dispatch, `capabilities/uniqueness.rs` owns duplicate package capability diagnostics and seen-capability insertion, `capability_dependencies/dependencies.rs` owns the package dependency entry point, `dependencies/rows.rs` owns dependency row traversal, `dependencies/rows/pairs.rs` owns seen `(id, capability)` pair state lifetime, `dependencies/row.rs` owns dependency row dispatch order, `dependencies/row/provider.rs` owns dependency provider token validation, `dependencies/row/capability.rs` owns required-capability adapter dispatch, `dependencies/row/pair.rs` owns duplicate dependency-pair adapter dispatch, `dependencies/capability.rs` owns required-capability dispatch, `dependencies/capability/presence.rs` owns missing required-capability diagnostics, `dependencies/capability/namespace.rs` owns dependency capability namespace diagnostics, and `dependencies/pairs.rs` owns duplicate dependency-pair diagnostics plus seen insertion. Asset-importer package validation follows the same split: `package_validation/asset_importers.rs` is the entry point, `asset_importers/rows.rs` owns row traversal, `asset_importers/rows/state.rs` owns seen-id state creation, `asset_importers/row.rs` owns per-importer identity and required-capability dispatch, `asset_importers/identity.rs` owns the identity entry point, `asset_importers/identity/metadata.rs` owns metadata rule dispatch, `asset_importers/identity/metadata/namespace.rs` owns importer-id namespace diagnostics, `asset_importers/identity/metadata/owner.rs` owns owner plugin-id token and package-match diagnostics, `asset_importers/identity/metadata/version.rs` owns positive importer-version diagnostics, `asset_importers/identity/uniqueness.rs` owns package-local duplicate-id diagnostics, `asset_importers/required_capabilities.rs` owns required-capability traversal and rule dispatch, `asset_importers/required_capabilities/namespace.rs` owns required-capability namespace diagnostics, `asset_importers/required_capabilities/state.rs` owns seen-required-capability state creation, and `asset_importers/required_capabilities/uniqueness.rs` owns duplicate required-capability diagnostics plus seen insertion. The static manifest contract mirror is folder-backed too: `static_manifest_contracts/asset_importers.rs` keeps child-module wiring only, `asset_importers/rows.rs` owns the full static asset-importer row contract, `asset_importers/traversal.rs` owns optional `asset_importers` table traversal, `asset_importers/ids.rs` owns dot-namespace importer-id assertions and the focused public id test, `asset_importers/kinds.rs` owns known `ResourceKind` output validation, and `asset_importers/selectors.rs` owns source-extension/full-suffix selector normalization checks.

Package layout validation follows the same folder-backed shape: `package_validation/layout.rs` owns the public metadata, coordinate, supported-target/platform, and root orchestration order, `layout/public_metadata.rs` owns package category validation plus description-rule dispatch, `layout/description.rs` owns optional description trim diagnostics, `layout/supported_targets.rs` owns supported-target traversal, `layout/supported_targets/state.rs` owns seen-target state creation, `layout/supported_targets/uniqueness.rs` owns duplicate supported-target detection and seen-target insertion, `layout/supported_platforms.rs` owns supported-platform traversal, `layout/supported_platforms/state.rs` owns seen-platform state creation, and `layout/supported_platforms/uniqueness.rs` owns duplicate supported-platform detection and seen-platform insertion before package rows enter catalog availability or export planning.

Package coordinate validation follows the same folder-backed shape: `package_validation/coordinates.rs` owns the internal coordinate validation entry point, `coordinates/fields.rs` owns coordinate presence gating and shape dispatch, `coordinates/presence.rs` owns presence orchestration, `coordinates/presence/fields.rs` owns borrowed coordinate field declaration state, `coordinates/presence/completeness.rs` owns the all-or-none `package_prefix` / `package_company` / `package_name` completeness diagnostic, `coordinates/shape.rs` owns coordinate shape dispatch, `coordinates/shape/prefix.rs` owns reverse-DNS prefix segment validation, and `coordinates/shape/segment.rs` owns company/name lowercase segment diagnostics.

Capability-status package validation is folder-backed as well. `package_validation/capability_status.rs` owns the internal entry point, `capability_status/owned_capabilities.rs` collects package and optional-feature-owned capabilities, `capability_status/identity.rs` owns identity-rule dispatch, `capability_status/identity/namespace.rs` owns per-status capability namespace diagnostics, `capability_status/identity/ownership.rs` owns package-owned capability diagnostics, `capability_status/identity/uniqueness.rs` owns duplicate-row diagnostics, `capability_status/rows.rs` owns row traversal, `capability_status/rows/state.rs` owns seen-capability state creation, `capability_status/row.rs` owns per-status validation order, `capability_status/row/identity.rs` owns the identity adapter, `capability_status/row/targets.rs` owns the target-coverage adapter, `capability_status/row/references.rs` owns the Bevy source-reference adapter, `capability_status/row/note.rs` owns the note adapter, and `capability_status/note.rs` owns optional note field diagnostics.

Capability-status target validation stays folder-backed as well. `package_validation/capability_status_targets.rs` owns the internal entry point, `capability_status_targets/rows.rs` owns status target-mode traversal, `capability_status_targets/rows/state.rs` owns seen target state creation, `capability_status_targets/row.rs` owns per-target validation order, `capability_status_targets/row/uniqueness.rs` owns duplicate-target adapter dispatch, `capability_status_targets/row/coverage.rs` owns package supported-target coverage adapter dispatch, `capability_status_targets/uniqueness.rs` owns duplicate target diagnostics, and `capability_status_targets/coverage.rs` owns package supported-target coverage diagnostics. This keeps target-mode row bookkeeping separate from package-level capability-status ownership.

Capability-status Bevy reference validation stays folder-backed as well. `package_validation/capability_status_references.rs` owns the internal entry point, `capability_status_references/rows.rs` owns Bevy reference list traversal, `capability_status_references/rows/state.rs` owns seen-reference state creation, `capability_status_references/row.rs` owns per-reference validation order, `capability_status_references/row/field.rs` owns package field adapter dispatch, `capability_status_references/row/path.rs` owns repository path adapter dispatch, `capability_status_references/row/uniqueness.rs` owns per-capability uniqueness adapter dispatch, `capability_status_references/field.rs` owns package field diagnostics, `capability_status_references/path.rs` owns repository-relative `dev/bevy` path shape dispatch, `capability_status_references/path/segments.rs` owns empty/current/parent segment diagnostics, and `capability_status_references/uniqueness.rs` owns duplicate reference diagnostics. This keeps source-traceability rows strict without letting the package-validation owner grow into field validation, path parsing, segment validation, or per-row duplicate bookkeeping.

Embedded-feature package validation follows the same owner/child shape. `package_validation/embedded_features.rs` owns package-level entry plus package-kind dispatch order, `embedded_features/kind.rs` owns `package_kind` match dispatch, `embedded_features/kind/standard.rs` owns Standard package feature-extension rejection, `embedded_features/kind/feature_extension.rs` owns FeatureExtension package required-provider and optional-feature mixing diagnostics, `embedded_features/lists.rs` owns list-kind dispatch, `embedded_features/lists/state.rs` owns the shared provider seen-state factory and lifetime type, `embedded_features/lists/optional.rs` owns optional-feature row traversal, `embedded_features/lists/feature_extension.rs` owns feature-extension row traversal, `embedded_features/row.rs` owns per-feature dispatch order, `embedded_features/manifest.rs` owns runtime feature-manifest validation dispatch into the shared feature validator, `embedded_features/row/provider.rs` owns provider validation delegation, and `embedded_features/row/target_coverage.rs` owns target-coverage delegation. Provider validation is nested under `embedded_feature_providers.rs`: `embedded_feature_providers/provider_id.rs` owns provider package-id selection, `embedded_feature_providers/uniqueness.rs` owns duplicate provider diagnostics, `embedded_feature_targets.rs` owns the internal target-coverage entry point, `embedded_feature_targets/modules.rs` owns feature module traversal, `embedded_feature_targets/module.rs` owns per-module target-mode dispatch, and `embedded_feature_targets/coverage.rs` owns per-target coverage against package `supported_targets`.

Package contribution validation follows the same shape before registry installation. `package_validation/contributions.rs` owns the internal entry point, while `contributions/groups.rs` owns manifest-local duplicate and package-owner validation group dispatch. The duplicate and owner implementations stay in their existing folder-backed owners, so the contribution entry point no longer grows with every contribution row family.

Option duplicate validation is nested one level deeper: `contribution_duplicates/options.rs` owns option row traversal, `contribution_duplicates/options/state.rs` owns seen-key state creation, and `contribution_duplicates/options/uniqueness.rs` owns duplicate option-key diagnostics plus seen insertion. Event catalog duplicate validation follows the same nested shape: `contribution_duplicates/event_catalogs.rs` owns event catalog row traversal, `contribution_duplicates/event_catalogs/state.rs` owns seen-namespace state creation, and `contribution_duplicates/event_catalogs/uniqueness.rs` owns duplicate event-catalog namespace diagnostics plus seen insertion. Component duplicate validation now mirrors that split: `contribution_duplicates/components.rs` owns component row traversal, `contribution_duplicates/components/state.rs` owns seen-type state creation, and `contribution_duplicates/components/uniqueness.rs` owns duplicate component type-id diagnostics plus seen insertion. UI component duplicate validation mirrors it as well: `contribution_duplicates/ui_components.rs` owns UI component row traversal, `contribution_duplicates/ui_components/state.rs` owns seen-id state creation, and `contribution_duplicates/ui_components/uniqueness.rs` owns duplicate UI component id diagnostics plus seen insertion. Event catalog owner validation is nested under `contribution_owners/event_catalogs.rs`, which owns event catalog row traversal, `contribution_owners/event_catalogs/prefix.rs` owns expected package-prefix construction, and `contribution_owners/event_catalogs/ownership.rs` owns namespace package-prefix diagnostics. Component owner validation is nested under `contribution_owners/components.rs`, which owns component row traversal, while `contribution_owners/components/ownership.rs` owns component `plugin_id` ownership diagnostics. UI component owner validation mirrors the same split: `contribution_owners/ui_components.rs` owns UI component row traversal, while `contribution_owners/ui_components/ownership.rs` owns UI component `plugin_id` ownership diagnostics.

`RuntimePluginCatalog` keeps the public aggregation type and storage in `runtime_plugin_catalog.rs`, while its public method families live under the folder-backed catalog owner. Registration and construction are in `registration.rs`, package and diagnostics accessors are in `access.rs`, project manifest projection is in `project.rs`, feature dependency reporting is in `feature_dependencies.rs`, runtime extension report projection is in `runtime_extensions.rs`, and the success predicate is in `status.rs`. Lower-level feature definition, selection, resolution, extension merge, and project report modules stay as sibling behavior files, so new catalog projections can be added without expanding the owner declaration. Feature definition collection keeps package-declared rows in `feature_definition_collection/package.rs`, while `feature_definition_collection.rs` owns package/runtime merge ordering and final `FeatureDefinitionMap` assembly. Runtime feature-definition merge is grouped under `runtime_feature_definitions/`: `merge.rs` owns runtime registration ordering, `registration.rs` owns single registration duplicate detection and definition insertion, `conflict.rs` owns package-declaration conflict projection, while `registration_match.rs` owns package-manifest registration equality checks. Feature resolution is grouped under `feature_resolution/`: `availability.rs` owns the fixed-point loop, `availability/outcome.rs` owns available/blocked/waiting status projection, `feature_blocking.rs` owns final blocked-feature projection, and `feature_blocking/cycle.rs` owns unresolved feature id collection plus cycle marking. Feature status projection is grouped under `feature_status/`: the root file owns owner/provider/target status orchestration, while `dependencies.rs` owns dependency-row missing-plugin and missing-capability accumulation. Feature dependency report context is grouped under `features/`: `features.rs` owns report flow, while `context.rs` owns plugin-selection lookup, enabled package set, base capability seed, and initial report construction. Project manifest completion is grouped under `project_manifest/`: `completion.rs` owns manifest construction orchestration, `selection_defaults.rs` owns defaulting orchestration, `selection_defaults/catalog_selections.rs` owns missing package selection insertion, `selection_defaults/hydration.rs` owns runtime/editor crate plus target-mode hydration, and `lookup.rs` owns catalog package selection lookup. Project extension reports are grouped under `project_extension_report/`: `enabled_packages.rs` owns project-enabled package id projection, `runtime_merge.rs` owns enabled runtime package extension merging, `feature_diagnostics.rs` owns feature dependency diagnostic projection, and `feature_merge.rs` owns available feature extension merge. Contribution fan-in follows the same shape: `contributions.rs` is the structural owner, `contributions/extension.rs` owns runtime extension registry merge orchestration, and `contributions/diagnostic.rs` owns registry-result diagnostic projection shared by render and descriptor contribution groups. Descriptor fan-in is grouped under `descriptor_contributions/`: `component.rs` owns component/UI component registration, `plugin_metadata.rs` owns option and event catalog rows, and `asset_scene.rs` owns asset importer plus scene-hook rows. Render fan-in is grouped under `render_contributions/`: `feature.rs` owns render feature and render pass executor rows, `prepare.rs` owns runtime prepare collectors, and `providers.rs` owns Virtual Geometry, Hybrid GI, and Solari runtime providers.

`EditorPlugin` mirrors that shape on the editor side. It exposes an `EditorPluginDescriptor`, attaches editor module metadata to the runtime package manifest, publishes editor capabilities, and provides `register_editor_extensions(...)` for views, drawers, menu items, component drawers, UI templates, and editor operations. `EditorPluginRegistrationReport::from_plugin(...)` is the single-plugin editor host-facing collection result: it carries the combined package manifest, capability list, editor extension registry, and diagnostics. Linked editor reports enter the live event/runtime layer through `EditorEventRuntime::register_editor_plugin_registration(...)`, which wraps the registry in an `EditorExtensionRegistration` and binds the report capabilities to contributed workbench view and editor operation descriptors. Native editor registration follows the same report shape, but only packages that declare editor modules or editor capabilities are converted into editor registration reports, and their registration package manifest is projected to editor modules before entering the editor extension registry. Runtime-only native packages remain selectable/exportable without polluting the editor extension registry. The manager keeps those descriptors registered, while descriptor listing, open, restore, reflection menu projection, component drawer lookup, UI template lookup, operation discovery, and operation invocation filter through the current `EditorCapabilitySnapshot`; disabled plugin surfaces are therefore hidden and cannot be opened or invoked, and enabling the capability makes the same registration report visible without rebuilding the editor shell. `EditorPluginCatalog` aggregates editor plugin reports into package manifests, deduplicated capabilities, a merged editor extension registry, and diagnostics for the plugin window. When a descriptor has no runtime package manifest, the catalog now creates a standalone editor-host package manifest with the descriptor category and package-level editor capabilities before adding the editor module row. `EditorManager::plugin_catalog()` now composes the builtin runtime and editor plugin catalogs, so plugin-window and export consumers no longer need to hand-assemble package metadata. `EditorManager::plugin_status_report(...)` produces the plugin-window status model from the project manifest plus both catalogs, including enabled/required state, target modes, packaging strategy, runtime/editor crates, runtime/editor capabilities, and per-plugin diagnostics. `EditorManager::complete_project_plugin_manifest(...)` applies catalog defaults before export planning, so generated source templates and library-embed builds do not depend on every crate name being handwritten in `zircon-project.toml`. `EditorManager::set_project_plugin_enabled(...)` is the unified builtin plugin toggle: it updates the project plugin selection from `RuntimePluginCatalog`, applies editor capabilities from `EditorPluginCatalog`, refreshes the capability snapshot, and returns an `EditorPluginEnableReport` for UI diagnostics. `EditorManager::set_project_plugin_packaging(...)` and `set_project_plugin_target_modes(...)` update the same project selection without changing editor capability enablement, returning `EditorPluginSelectionUpdateReport` so editor controls can alter release policy independently from plugin visibility. The native-aware variants perform the same updates after completing the manifest with external native package selections. The editor plugin crate may depend on `zircon_editor`; the runtime plugin crate continues to depend only on runtime contracts.

The minimal module plugin window is now a concrete Slint pane instead of a placeholder view. `editor.module_plugins` resolves to `ViewContentKind::ModulePlugins`; the host projects `EditorPluginStatusReport` into `ModulePluginsPaneData`, displays builtin and native-discovered packages with enabled/required state, target modes, packaging strategy, crate names, runtime capabilities, editor capabilities, and diagnostics, and routes Enable/Disable button clicks through `EditorManager::set_native_aware_project_plugin_enabled(...)`. The same action path now exposes policy buttons for cycling packaging strategy and target mode presets; those actions call `set_native_aware_project_plugin_packaging(...)` and `set_native_aware_project_plugin_target_modes(...)`, then save the changed selection back to `zircon-project.toml`. Successful toggles and policy edits mark layout and presentation dirty so capability-gated view lists and plugin status rows refresh immediately. The pane body is scrollable, which keeps the full builtin/native catalog reachable as plugin packages move out of the editor core.

`PluginPackageManifest` now records public package metadata before contribution data: `sdk_api_version`, package `category`, `supported_targets`, `supported_platforms`, package-level `capabilities`, `asset_roots`, `content_roots`, dependency declarations, option/event/importer catalogs, optional feature bundles, feature-extension provider bundles, package `package_kind`, and `default_packaging`. Module declarations now cover runtime, editor, native, and VM module kinds, while descriptor-derived manifests copy runtime target modes and package capabilities from `RuntimePluginDescriptor` instead of relying on a second hand-written catalog. This keeps export planning separate from editor-only tooling while still letting a package advertise the ECS, runtime UI, importer, NativeDynamic, and future VM surfaces it contributes.

Package root validation is now folder-backed under `package_validation/roots.rs`: the owner keeps `asset_roots` / `content_roots` orchestration, `roots/array.rs` owns per-array row traversal and path validation dispatch, `roots/array/state.rs` owns seen-root state creation, `roots/array/uniqueness.rs` owns duplicate root diagnostics and seen-root insertion, `roots/path.rs` owns root path rule dispatch, `roots/path/field.rs` owns non-empty/trimmed diagnostics, `roots/path/relative.rs` owns relative-path diagnostics, `roots/path/separator.rs` owns forward-slash diagnostics, and `roots/path/segments.rs` owns empty/current/parent segment diagnostics. This keeps export-package path policy local to the manifest validation subtree without mixing it back into package layout metadata or contribution validation.

The package manifest implementation is split by responsibility: module-kind declarations, module contribution declarations, package declarations, constructors, and the built-in package catalog live in separate files. `package_manifest/mod.rs` remains a structural re-export boundary only.

Plugin component ids are full dotted names such as `weather.Component.CloudLayer`. They are intentionally not collapsed into a special `World` branch. `World` stores dynamic plugin component payloads per entity, exposes them through `dynamic_component(...)` and `dynamic_components_for_entity(...)`, and routes property reads/writes through the same `ComponentPropertyPath` surface used by built-in components. Built-in components keep their existing high-performance maps; plugin components sit beside them for serialization, inspector property enumeration, and editor mutation.

`ComponentTypeRegistry` is now the scene-side type layer for plugin component descriptors. A world can register `ComponentTypeDescriptor` values from runtime plugin contributions, reject duplicate component type ids, and validate that registered ids stay under the contributing plugin namespace. `RuntimeExtensionRegistry::apply_component_types_to_world(...)` is the shared install point from plugin registration reports into an active `World`, so native Rust plugins and future VM host handles converge on the same ECS type table before entity attachment. When the registry is populated, attaching or property-writing an unregistered dynamic component is rejected; an empty registry remains permissive for legacy scene data and transitional tests. If a registered descriptor declares properties, `World::set_property(...)` also enforces that dynamic component writes target a declared property and that the property is marked `editable`; descriptors with no property list remain schemaless for early plugin experiments. `DynamicComponentInstance` pairs stored JSON payloads with the currently loaded descriptor when one exists; when a plugin schema is absent, the payload is still enumerable so editor Inspector views can show a protected degraded row instead of deleting serialized component data. The registry itself remains runtime metadata, while `DynamicScene` now serializes the subset of plugin component descriptors required by captured plugin-owned dynamic components and installs them into target worlds before scene apply.

Runtime UI component contributions now install into the same `UiComponentDescriptorRegistry` used by engine UI component metadata. `RuntimeExtensionRegistry::apply_ui_components_to_registry(...)` converts the lightweight plugin declaration `{ component_id, plugin_id, ui_document }` into a runtime `UiComponentDescriptor` with `plugin-ui-component` role, `plugin_id` / `ui_document` required props, and a generic `content` slot. Registration rejects legacy `.ui.toml` and `.v2.ui.toml` documents; plugin-authored UI component documents must be single-component `.zui` assets backed by the v2 prototype/cache payload path.

The current dynamic component payload is `serde_json::Value` so VM and native plugin hosts can feed the same storage through stable data handles. Scalar writes use the engine `ScenePropertyValue` conversion path, which keeps editor field edits and serialized dynamic component data aligned.

## Runtime Validation Notes

The focused runtime coverage now checks three contracts:

- registry collection and duplicate diagnostics for managers, modules, scene hooks, render features, render pass executors, Virtual Geometry runtime providers, components, UI components, plugin options, plugin event catalogs, and asset importers, including programmatic module descriptor field validation, manager contributor plugin-id validation, scene-hook id/plugin-id shape validation, and catalog-level duplicate manager merge diagnostics
- plugin component descriptor field/property validation, component/UI component plugin-id token validation, plugin UI component id prefix and `.zui` document validation, registration-report diagnostics for invalid package-manifest component/UI rows even when the same ids were already registered directly, plus `UiComponentDescriptorRegistry` installation
- package manifest round-trip of public package metadata, runtime/editor/native/VM module declarations, importer descriptors, optional feature bundles, component declarations, and UI component declarations
- package-manifest option keys, event-catalog namespaces, component type ids, and UI component ids are diagnosed for manifest-local duplicates before valid direct-registration mirrors are ignored
- package-manifest event catalog namespaces, component plugin ids, and UI component plugin ids are diagnosed when they are owned by a different package id; package option keys stay namespace-only because they may use domain namespaces distinct from package ids
- all static component and UI component package rows are guarded for known top-level/nested fields, package-owned lowercase dot-namespaced ids, matching contributor `plugin_id`, globally unique ids, trimmed display fields, duplicate-free component property names, boolean `editable` property markers, and relative forward-slash `.zui` UI component documents before plugin windows, runtime registries, or scene/UI catalogs consume static package metadata
- package-manifest asset importer rows are registry-validated before duplicate direct-registration mirrors are ignored, so invalid shadowed importer metadata still reaches registration reports and runtime catalog diagnostics; native package manifests use the same contribution path and no longer pre-register importer rows separately
- runtime package-manifest asset importer rows also receive production diagnostics for importer id namespace shape, package owner, positive importer version, package-local duplicate ids, and required-capability shape/uniqueness before catalog projection
- SourceTemplate generation of linked runtime crate `plugin_registration()` calls and `EntryRunner::bootstrap_with_runtime_plugin_registrations(...)`
- app startup acceptance of a required external runtime plugin when a linked registration report contributes the requested module
- app startup rejection of a linked registration report whose target modes do not match the requested runtime target
- descriptor-derived runtime plugin package ids, display names, and `zircon_plugin_` crate names are diagnosed before registration reports and runtime catalogs consume them
- runtime plugin descriptor/package manifest default packaging presence and uniqueness, package manifest ids, display names, package/API versions, and linked descriptor/package id agreement are diagnosed before registration reports and runtime catalogs consume package metadata; descriptor `package_id` and package manifest `id` also follow the static package id shape contract of lowercase leading letters with no trailing or repeated underscores
- runtime plugin package capabilities are diagnosed as non-empty lowercase dot namespaces with package-local uniqueness before capability statuses, dependency reports, native-loader registration reports, and runtime catalogs consume package metadata
- runtime plugin package module rows are diagnosed for package-owned names, runtime/editor suffixes, crate-name shape including no trailing or repeated underscores, capability shape, target-mode coverage, editor target restrictions, and package-local duplicate names before registration reports and runtime catalogs consume package metadata
- runtime plugin feature manifest ids, static-shaped owner/dependency/provider package tokens, display names, capabilities, dependencies, feature module crate-name shape including no trailing or repeated underscores, and default packaging presence/uniqueness are diagnosed before feature registration reports and runtime catalogs consume them
- project manifest plugin selection `id` values are diagnosed during export build-plan projection with the same package-token shape before linked runtime availability, native dynamic package manifests, or fatal export diagnostics consume them
- project manifest optional-feature `id` values are diagnosed during export build-plan projection with the same owner-prefixed lowercase dot namespace shape before external feature provider links, generated registration calls, unknown-feature suppression, or fatal export diagnostics consume them
- project manifest optional-feature `provider_package_id` overrides are diagnosed during export build-plan projection with the same package-token shape before external feature provider links, generated registration calls, or fatal export diagnostics consume them
- project manifest plugin and optional-feature `runtime_crate` overrides are diagnosed during export build-plan projection with the same `zircon_plugin_` crate-name shape before linked runtime crate lists, generated Cargo dependencies, generated registration calls, or generated SourceTemplate project selection metadata consume them; explicit `editor_crate` overrides use the same project-side crate-name diagnostics and are scrubbed from generated project selection metadata when malformed
- runtime plugin package-embedded `optional_features` and `feature_extensions` reuse feature manifest diagnostics, reject duplicate feature/provider rows, and require feature module target modes to be covered by package `supported_targets` before catalog feature definitions and project selections consume them
- plugin workspace runtime-package behavior tests for physics, sound, net, navigation, particles, texture, animation, virtual geometry, hybrid GI, and Solari, including render feature pass/capability metadata for advanced and experimental render plugins
- runtime/editor builtin catalog consistency tests proving catalog entries have matching `plugin.toml` files and `zircon_plugins` workspace members, including `physics` and `animation`
- descriptor-derived package manifests for runtime plugins preserve the target modes declared in each package `plugin.toml`

- all static optional feature rows are guarded for matching owner plugin id, globally unique owner-namespaced lowercase dot-separated feature id that does not collide with package ids, non-empty display name/capabilities, known default packaging strategies, and explicit `enabled_by_default`; static package manifests also treat omitted `package_kind` as `standard`, recognize `feature_extensions` row tables as `PluginFeatureBundleManifest` rows, reject Standard packages that declare feature-extension rows, and require `feature_extension` packages to declare feature-extension rows without mixing in optional-feature rows
- static feature-extension provider rows now use provider-aware row contracts: the feature id stays under `owner_plugin_id` instead of the provider package id, owner/dependency plugin ids use lowercase package tokens, dependencies carry exactly one primary owner dependency and duplicate-free plugin/capability pairs, known static dependency capabilities must resolve, runtime/editor provider modules stay under the feature namespace with kind-matching suffixes, `zircon_plugin_` crate names, kind-matching capabilities, known package-covered target modes, and editor-only `editor_host` targets, and shared static module traversal now also resolves provider module crates against `zircon_plugins/Cargo.toml` while requiring them to stay under the declaring provider package root
- runtime feature registration reports diagnose malformed programmatic feature ids, static-shaped owner/dependency/provider package tokens, display names, empty/invalid/duplicate capabilities, malformed dependency rows, and empty or duplicate default packaging before feature selection or export planning consumes them, while runtime package registration reports apply the same feature manifest diagnostics to embedded `optional_features` / `feature_extensions`, add package-local provider uniqueness plus package target coverage checks, and enforce `package_kind` coherence for Standard versus FeatureExtension package feature rows
- all static optional-feature and feature-extension dependency rows are guarded for non-empty trimmed lowercase provider `plugin_id` and capability, explicit primary marker, exactly one primary dependency pointing back to the owning package capability, and feature-local unique plugin/capability pairs; runtime feature registration reports enforce the feature-local dependency shape, primary count, owner primary id, and duplicate plugin/capability pairs before catalog-level dependency resolution checks capability ownership
- dependency capability references that point at a known static package must resolve to a capability declared by that package, one of its optional features, or one of its feature-extension provider rows; dependency rows without a static package target must use host-owned `runtime.module.*` or `runtime.capability.*` namespaces, and option/importer capability gates must resolve to declared static package/feature/feature-extension capabilities or explicit host-owned capabilities
- all static package, optional-feature, and feature-extension module rows are guarded for non-empty identity fields (`name`, known `kind`, and `crate_name`), manifest-local unique lowercase dot-separated module names, non-empty string capabilities, and known target modes before loader, plugin-window, availability, and export projections consume module-local metadata
- all static module names are guarded under their package or optional-feature namespace, feature-extension provider module names are guarded under their feature namespace, and every module `crate_name` is resolved against `zircon_plugins/Cargo.toml` workspace member package names while requiring the resolved member path to stay under the declaring package root
- static runtime/editor module names must end with matching `.runtime` or `.editor` suffixes, so module identity remains readable before loader, plugin-window, availability, and export projections consume module-local metadata
- all static package, optional-feature, and feature-extension module target modes must be covered by the declaring package's `supported_targets`, so package availability cannot be narrower than module availability
- static editor module rows must only advertise `editor_host` target modes, so editor-only crates cannot be projected into client or server runtime availability or export planning
- static runtime/editor module capability namespaces must match module kind (`runtime.*` for runtime modules and `editor.*` for editor modules), so module-local capability rows cannot be projected through the wrong host surface
- Solari static package metadata, linked runtime package metadata, and descriptor-derived catalog metadata preserve category `rendering`, maturity `experimental`, client/editor targets, runtime capabilities, and the partial status note for the unavailable realtime pass executor
- NativeDynamic fixture static metadata and embedded native-library metadata preserve SDK category, experimental maturity, client/server/editor targets, package-level runtime/editor capabilities, and `NativeDynamic` default packaging before the loader reads the embedded TOML
- descriptor-derived project selections preserve non-LibraryEmbed default packaging when the descriptor only advertises another export strategy such as `NativeDynamic`
- runtime-only native loading filters out editor-only packages before dynamic-library probing and does not invoke editor entry functions during runtime export startup
- native editor registration filters out runtime-only packages and uses editor-only native loading while preserving runtime-only packages for plugin status and export selection
- split native packages use the runtime module crate for runtime-only loading, the editor module crate for editor-only loading, and both target libraries during full diagnostic status loading
- native-aware export validation probes exported dynamic packages with the loader matching the export profile target mode
- runtime plugin registration reports keep editor entry diagnostics out of runtime startup diagnostics
- native runtime/editor registration paths use target-specific diagnostic report APIs instead of the full plugin-window diagnostic surface
- native diagnostic report coverage asserts that full diagnostics include both runtime/editor entry messages while target-specific projections include only their matching entry kind
- native runtime registration reports strip editor module declarations before entering runtime startup
- native editor registration reports strip runtime module declarations before entering the editor extension registry
- NativeDynamic materialization sanitizes output directories, refuses direct package-id source lookup when the id contains path components, and blocks mutating generated-file/package copy output while export build-plan fatal diagnostics are present
- mobile and browser export targets reject `NativeDynamic` packaging with fatal diagnostics, carry `ExportPlatformPolicy` in the build plan, and keep native load manifests out of Android/iOS/WebGPU/WASM plans
- SourceTemplate mobile/browser export targets emit package manifests and packaging scripts for Android Gradle, iOS Swift Package, WebGPU Vite, and WASM Vite hosts through `source_template_emits_package_manifests_for_mobile_and_browser_hosts`
- SourceTemplate mobile/browser release contracts emit Android signing/Play metadata, iOS export/App Store Connect metadata, and WebGPU/WASM CDN cache/deploy metadata through `source_template_emits_signing_and_cdn_release_contracts`
  - SourceTemplate mobile/browser generated hosts translate Android/iOS/browser lifecycle, touch/pointer, keyboard, viewport metrics, and browser fetch callbacks through `generated_mobile_and_browser_hosts_translate_platform_callbacks_to_runtime_abi_events`
  - SourceTemplate Android/iOS hosts emit repo-owned JNI/Swift binding headers plus mobile resource-map files through `generated_platform_hosts_include_repo_owned_binding_and_resource_glue`
  - SourceTemplate WebGPU/WASM hosts instantiate generated WASM exports, forward browser callbacks to exported ABI symbols, and reject resource fetches outside the declared asset root through `generated_browser_hosts_instantiate_wasm_exports_and_gate_asset_origins`
- Generated release adapters require real store/CDN credential inputs and perform upload/hash/precompression handoff through `generated_release_adapters_gate_real_store_and_cdn_upload_inputs`
- `zircon_runtime_interface` ABI contract tests now cover lifecycle, touch, keyboard, viewport metrics, streaming host fetch DTOs, and `ZrRuntimeTranslatedEventV1` host translation helpers
- the CI export-platform contract matrix validates desktop, mobile, and browser platform policy rows through `ZR_EXPORT_CONTRACT_PLATFORM`
- SourceTemplate export generation from `ExportBuildPlan::from_project_manifest(...)` completes builtin catalog selections before projection, preserving catalog runtime crate names and target modes in generated `src/zircon_plugins.rs` project plugin selections
- renderer feature asset behavior for descriptor-owned plugin features, duplicate plugin feature names, and capability-driven advanced runtime flags
- default forward/deferred pipeline assets excluding pluginized advanced VG/GI/particle built-in feature slots at construction time, with legacy built-in behavior isolated to explicit test fixtures
- runtime submission and scene renderer runtime flags ignoring legacy VG/GI built-in identity unless the compiled descriptor declares the matching capability requirement
- render pass executor registry coverage excluding VG/GI/particle from the base built-in executor list and accepting those executor ids only through linked plugin render descriptors
- pipeline asset registration/reload rejection of plugin executor ids when the asset declares them behind quality or capability gates but the framework was not constructed with the matching linked render descriptor, plus acceptance when the descriptor is linked
- app startup render submission with a linked `virtual_geometry` registration report, proving contributed render feature descriptors rebuild the default pipeline and replace the old core virtual-geometry pass list before execution stats are recorded
- app startup render submission without linked advanced render plugins, proving a VG/GI quality request no longer executes old core advanced passes from the base pipeline
- app startup render submission with an explicitly registered legacy built-in pipeline fixture, proving VG/GI quality flags only open capability-gated plugin descriptors and do not reopen old `BuiltinRenderFeature` pass lists
- scene `ComponentTypeRegistry` duplicate/type-prefix diagnostics and dynamic component attachment gate when registered descriptors are present
- RuntimeExtensionRegistry component id prefix, field trim, and duplicate-property validation plus World ComponentTypeRegistry installation
- `editor_ui_11_m5_runtime_extension_component_zui_guard_passed` records the runtime extension registry UI component fixture cleanup: `tools/tests/test_zui_static_suffix_convergence.py::test_runtime_extension_component_tests_use_zui_suffix` scans `zircon_runtime/src/tests/plugin_extensions/extension_registry_components.rs`; `runtime_extension_registry_rejects_non_zui_ui_component_documents` still uses `UiComponentDescriptor::new` through `register_ui_component` to reject non-`.zui` UI component documents, but the invalid fixture is now ordinary `.toml` input instead of retired `.ui.toml` / `.v2.ui.toml` suffixes. This keeps the runtime extension registry contract `.zui`-only without restoring retired suffix loader/importer compatibility.
- dynamic plugin component attach, world serialization round-trip, property read, editable property write, readonly write rejection, undeclared property rejection, descriptor-backed enumeration, and descriptor-missing protected enumeration through existing scene property paths
- plugin unload guard behavior: `World::ensure_plugin_components_can_unload(plugin_id)` rejects unload while any entity still owns a dynamic component whose id starts with that plugin namespace, and succeeds after those components are removed
- target-client module ordering keeps GraphicsBase inside the minimal runtime core and appends UI as the default client plugin, matching the compile-time feature split
